ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-07-sejm-22-3.u564	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym of the Republic of Poland! On 11 July 1943, Ukrainian nationalists attacked 99 villages in Wołyń, mainly in Horochów, Włodzimierz and Kowel. The assault had an organized and massive character. Thousands of our compatriots died that day, including children, women, and old men. The most frightening thing is that the victims were killed not only by bullets from firearms, but also by farm tools: pitchforks, axes and saws. The Ukrainian nationalists did not even go back to desecrating temples by attacking churches where the faithful prayed. Sunday was chosen by accident. For the criminals knew that Poles would go to Holy Mass on Sunday. In the following days, the Ukrainian Uprising Army continued its genocide against the Polish civilian population. Historians estimate that only in July 1943, Ukrainian nationalists attacked 520 villages, killing from 9 to 11 thousand. persons. They died because they were Poles. Bloody Sunday became a symbol of the genocide of the UPA. In fact, it was the culmination of crimes which began at the time of the entry into the former eastern provinces of the Republic of Poland and continued long after the end of World War II. The documented number of victims only in the Voivodship of Volynsk, Lviv, Stanisławowski and Tarnopol is almost 90 thousand. people, however, historians estimate that there could actually be 120 thousand. to 130 thousand. Victims. Moreover, these terrible events also covered Lubelskie and Poleskie voivodships. The crimes committed by UPA on the civilian population of Poland, as well as on Polish citizens of other nationalities, fulfil all the conditions of the definition of genocide that is a crime against humanity aimed at destroying in whole or in part nations, ethnic, religious or racially different groups. The cruelty of the perpetrators of this genocide has no equal in the history of the world. The most sophisticated and perverse methods of murder were applied to the most vulnerable victims: children, women and the elderly. They are documented, among others. cases of fetal killing taken out by cutting the mothers' stomachs, cutting the victims with saws, tearing bodies by dragging their legs through horses, as well as many hours of torture leading the victims to madness and finally to death. In this situation it is not understandable why the Sejm of the Republic on the eve of the 73rd anniversary of bloody Sunday did not adopt the Act establishing on 11 July the National Day of Remembrance of Victims of Genocide by the CNS-UPA on the citizens of Polish Eastern Kresów II of the Republic of Poland. Even the act of lower rank, which is the resolution of the Sejm, has not yet been taken on this matter. The leading politicians of the ruling party, speaking in the press about the Volyn slaughter, speak of a fratricidal war. This is not the way to tell anybody, especially parliamentarians. It was genocide. The pre-war policy of Henryk Józewski's voivodeship, which increased the number of only Ukrainian-speaking schools from 8 existing in 1930 to several hundred and supported the development of Ukrainian and Polish-Ukrainian social organizations, failed. Please, let's not go back to politics. We should cooperate and build an agreement with the Ukrainians, but not with the banners, as we built friendly relations with the Germans after the war, but not with the Nazis who were tried in Nuremberg. In Germany, no one's gonna think of buying monuments for Hitler. This should also be the case in Polish-Ukrainian relations. Poland must not allow acts such as the adoption by the Ukrainian parliament of a law paying tribute to UPA criminals in April 2015, and this during the visit to Kiev of the Polish President. It was a public provocation. The terrible war experiences of Poles, citizens of the Second Republic, in which Ukrainian nationalism was also blindly supported, and even trained fighters, future, as it turned out, bloody rezun, are unfortunately no lessons of history so far, from which the rulers would draw wisdom and foresight in directing the fate of the nation. How long will Poland and Poles be waiting for the establishment of a national holiday on 11 July in honor of the victims of genocide in Volyn, which is a symbol of the martyrdom of Polish citizens in the Eastern Kres? Please make this bill as soon as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
